Theorem 220.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For convergence analysis pairs (A,B) in computational theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 847 and the invariant I(A,B) = 425 mod 4.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to computational theory, we decompose the problem into 847 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 425. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.

Theorem 220.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the computational structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 735. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 690 and converges at rate O(1/n^4).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 735 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the computational structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 690. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 4. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
